Even if these countries do not have the necessary resources – even if they are still so economical, still doing so efficiently and carry out so many reforms – they do not have the resources to deal with the tasks of health care, the social systems, education, research and development which are becoming increasingly intensive for the countries of Europe. In Europe, no country has done well to reduce these taxes – and that is why the increase in capital taxes is so important. I know there are many who are against it. There are still some of them to convince. Even today, a Raiffeisen speaker has made a statement and troublesome. We do not problem these assets-related taxes, but we say that it is the right way to get more contributions from those in our country that can also afford more! [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Finally, I would like to say that, in many countries at present, very hard cuts are being made and in Austria we do not need to make comparable rigid, hard cuts, such as Germany, Italy, not to mention Portugal, Spain, Ireland, where the cuts cost many people their jobs, result in deep cuts in the social system, substantially increasing poverty. We don't need to do that in our country. However, let us also take advantage of the opportunities we have at this time, we are driving forward the reforms that are still ahead of us, and we see this budget as a building block, but far from being the last building block for the reforms in our country.

Individual positive measures are available in the budget. There is something there for thermal renovation, the climate fund is still there, the most has been changed. At the same time, however, exceptions were also immediately regulated – for example for the agricultural lobby and for the freight lobby – so that the environmental effects can be reversed immediately and unfortunately no environmental steering effects are achieved. In my view, it has been missed an opportunity to improve the Austrian environmental balance, which unfortunately is anything but valid in the future. In his budget speech, the Minister of Finance said: We can “obtain our plans for environmental and climate protection”. I ask the question: What plans? I am not really familiar with plans of the Austrian Federal Government in the environmental and climate fields. The energy strategy has run completely in the sand; There's no climate protection law. If what we now have is maintained, it sounds rather dangerous to me, because the Austrian climate balance is desperate. We are not close to our goal of achieving a savings of 13 percent on CO2, but we are putting out plus 11 percent. That means that we're not close to the end of a marathon if you're going to have this If you want to compare, the puste went out, but Austria has unfortunately been running in the wrong direction from the very beginning. We have a wild growth at the waste incinerators in Austria. If this is to be maintained, thank you. We cannot respect the limit values for different air pollutants. This cannot be the plan for environmental protection and climate protection. Debt not leaving the children was a big keyword, it has been quoted by many here. – Yes, I already mentioned it: If Austrian climate policy continues as it has so far, then future generations in Austria, children and young people will be given a proper burden, a burden that will have been washed. We see what is already causing negative effects through the lax climate change protection, which will be even stronger in the case of Weitem. From the budget for rehabilitating old loads, the Minister has turned something into his own budget. In other words, even there will be a shift in burdens into the next century that will have to be borne by future generations. I find that more than irresponsible. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] Performance fairness is an important keyword for the ÖVP. But I'm asking you: Is environmental pollution a performance? [[Abg. Head: What is this question?]] In 2011, the Ministry of the Environment provided €93 million for free pollution rights for the industry. If that's performance, thank you. In any case, the population is paying for the damage caused by this pollution. But it cannot be that it is free for the industry and it is also rewarded for it. I wonder at all why environmental pollution resources come from the environmental budget. The Minister for the Environment is no longer here. If this is paid by the Minister of Economic Affairs, it is also to be criticised, but to pay by means of environmental pollution from the Ministry of Environment, I think it is a scandal. A Minister for the Environment has to finance climate protection and environmental protection and not pollution. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] Unfortunately, the opportunity to move towards renewable energy towards jobs has been missed. Advertising is not an environmental protection. I therefore believe that Austria needs an independent, strong and committed Ministry of the Environment. Thank you.

Overall, of course, we welcome the Federal Government's investment in thermal renovation, because it has a very local impact because it stimulates value added in the regions, secures jobs and, because of its high demand efficiency, also promotes the commercial economy. Some will now ask themselves what to understand under the creative economy. This sector is divided into three groups according to the definition of US sociologist Richard Florida: These are the artists, the people in the Internet and communication industries, and those people who, with their companies, capture this creative potential and use it economically. This economic sector is the most growing sector and therefore belongs to support and demand. Does the budget of this group damage? - No, it doesn't! Does it put these mostly young self-employed insurmountable obstacles in the way? - No, it doesn't! Despite the savings package, Federal Minister Claudia Schmied was able to receive the funding pots for film, music and publicity, not only but also to provide better equipment on time. Film funding has been increased to €16.5 million, the funding for young artists of €600 000 will continue and the donation of the film and television agreement will be increased by €2.1 million. This is a clear sign that in Austria young artists and creative cultural creators are expressly desired. The increase in the annual budget of the Research Funding Society to € 51 million is also a start – a start to strengthen innovative research and innovation in the economy. Together with the funds of the Austrian Economic Service, about €70 million is available to support this area. The priorities of the funding set are hopeful for the future. In particular, 2011 will focus on the following areas: cooperation between industry and science, the financing and creation of innovative companies, cooperative research institutions, patent exploitation, international technology cooperation and the strengthening of human resources with specific women’s promotions. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] The budget could have been worse for the creative economy. I am pleased with the accents set, which, however, should not mean that everything is the way it should be. No, on the contrary! However, the foundation for future improvements is built, and it is now up to us to build on it.
